People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Yeah… there was a time like that, I guess. Right after I got fired, I didn’t feel crushed at first, weirdly. I felt kind of… fired up, like nobody could touch me, like maybe they’d done me a favor or something.

But I was also real quick to snap. If somebody said, “maybe slow down” or tried to calm me down, I’d get mad fast. My brother said something like that once while we were standing by his truck, and I just remember the sun was in my eyes and I was thinking about how dirty the windshield was instead of listening to him… but yeah, I got sharp with people. Like I couldn’t stand being told to ease up.

I end up just wanting to keep my head down.\n\nIt’s weird, ’cause I’ll think about stuff I used to do, or even just going outside, and then my mind wanders to other things, like the neighbors making noise or bills sitting there, and I just… I don’t know, I lose the point of doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities or things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of looking at the water, you know? And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces and all that… I’d sit there for hours sometimes.\n\nNow they’re just sitting there. I look at them like they belong to somebody else. Like, I know I used to care about them, but I can’t really get myself to start. And the lake too, I think about going, then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel depressed or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, it’s more like I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, it feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes I’ll just stare at the wall, and then I start thinking about stupid little things, like the mail piling up or how the floor needs sweeping, and then I forget what I was even trying to do. But yeah… most of the day I just want to keep my head down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this depressed or empty feeling for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been most days. I mean, I don’t keep a calendar or anything—my kitchen one’s still on the wrong month, actually—but when I wake up, it’s that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nAnd it kind of stays there, you know? I just keep my head down and get through the day, or try to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where you feel down or lose interest in things, along with problems like trouble sleeping, feeling tired, or thinking about yourself in a negative way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the sleep part, definitely. Lately I’ll go to bed and just lie there for hours, staring at the wall, like my body’s tired but it won’t shut off. There’s this little crack in the paint near the window I keep looking at, and then I start thinking I should fix it, but I don’t.\n\nOther times I wake up before the sun’s even up and I can’t get back to sleep. I just lay there waiting for the day to start, I guess. Like I couldn’t stand being told to ease up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I don’t keep a calendar or anything—my kitchen one’s still on the wrong month, actually—but when I wake up, it’s that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nAnd it kind of stays there, you know? I just keep my head down and get through the day, or try to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where you feel down or lose interest in things, along with problems like trouble sleeping, feeling tired, or thinking about yourself in a negative way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the sleep part, definitely. Lately I’ll go to bed and just lie there for hours, staring at the wall, like my body’s tired but it won’t shut off. But mostly, yeah… I’m not eating much, and I’m losing weight.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down, sad, or have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess down is one word for it. Most days when I wake up, it’s like there’s this heavy, empty feeling right here in my chest, like nothing good is waiting for me. I end up just wanting to keep my head down.\n\nIt’s weird, ’cause I’ll think about stuff I used to do, or even just going outside, and then my mind wanders to other things, like the neighbors making noise or bills sitting there, and I just… I don’t know, I lose the point of doing anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities or things you used to enjoy doing?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I used to like walking by the lake, just kind of looking at the water, you know? And I had these little model kits I’d work on, tiny pieces and all that… I’d sit there for hours sometimes.\n\nNow they’re just sitting there. I look at them like they belong to somebody else. Like, I know I used to care about them, but I can’t really get myself to start. And the lake too, I think about going, then I just… don’t.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that most days, you feel depressed or down most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, yeah. It’s not like I’m crying all day or anything, it’s more like I wake up and there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like before I even get out of bed, it feels like nothing good is waiting for me.\n\nSometimes I’ll just stare at the wall, and then I start thinking about stupid little things, like the mail piling up or how the floor needs sweeping, and then I forget what I was even trying to do. But yeah… most of the day I just want to keep my head down.\n\nDoctor: Have you had this depressed or empty feeling for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least the past two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been most days. I mean, I don’t keep a calendar or anything—my kitchen one’s still on the wrong month, actually—but when I wake up, it’s that same heavy, empty feeling in my chest. Like nothing good’s waiting for me.\n\nAnd it kind of stays there, you know? I just keep my head down and get through the day, or try to.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you’ve had days where you feel down or lose interest in things, along with problems like trouble sleeping, feeling tired, or thinking about yourself in a negative way?\n\nPatient: Yeah… the sleep part, definitely. Lately I’ll go to bed and just lie there for hours, staring at the wall, like my body’s tired but it won’t shut off. There’s this little crack in the paint near the window I keep looking at, and then I start thinking I should fix it, but I don’t.\n\nOther times I wake up before the sun’s even up and I can’t get back to sleep. I just lay there waiting for the day to start, I guess. And even when I do sleep some, I still feel worn out, like it didn’t count.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts raced, even if it was for just a few days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I guess. Right after I got fired, I didn’t feel crushed at first, weirdly. I felt kind of… fired up, like nobody could touch me, like maybe they’d done me a favor or something.\n\nBut I was also real quick to snap. If somebody said, “maybe slow down” or tried to calm me down, I’d get mad fast. My brother said something like that once while we were standing by his truck, and I just remember the sun was in my eyes and I was thinking about how dirty the windshield was instead of listening to him… but yeah, I got sharp with people. Like I couldn’t stand being told to ease up.\n\nDoctor: Did that fired-up, irritable period last for four days or longer, or did it fade more quickly?\n\nPatient: It didn’t just fade in a day or two, no. Maybe around there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had three or more separate periods in your life where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts raced?\n\nPatient: Three or more… I don’t know. There were times I got wound up or people said I was acting different, but whether that was three separate times… I can’t say for sure.\n\nI remember little pieces more than the whole thing, like where I was standing or what somebody said, but then the actual number of times gets fuzzy. So… I don’t know how to answer that cleanly.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were at least three different times in your life when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think of it like separate times, but my head doesn’t sort it that way. Like, I can remember being in certain places, or somebody looking at me funny, or me pacing around, but then whether that was one “time” or another “time”… I don’t know.\n\nThree sounds like a lot when you say it that way. Maybe? I really can’t say for sure. My memory kind of grabs onto weird little details instead of the count.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were at least three different times in your life when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I mean, the one I can really point to is right after I got fired. I keep thinking about that day and how people were looking at me, but as far as three different times… I still don’t know how to count it.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were at least three different times in your life when you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts were racing?\n\nPatient: I know I keep circling this, but the main one I can actually remember is right after I got fired. Lately I’ll go to bed and just lie there for hours, staring at the wall, like my body’s tired but it won’t shut off. There’s this little crack in the paint near the window I keep looking at, and then I start thinking I should fix it, but I don’t.\n\nOther times I wake up before the sun’s even up and I can’t get back to sleep. I just lay there waiting for the day to start, I guess. And even when I do sleep some, I still feel worn out, like it didn’t count.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had periods where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts raced, even if it was for just a few days?\n\nPatient: Yeah… there was a time like that, I guess. Right after I got fired, I didn’t feel crushed at first, weirdly. I felt kind of… fired up, like nobody could touch me, like maybe they’d done me a favor or something.\n\nBut I was also real quick to snap. If somebody said, “maybe slow down” or tried to calm me down, I’d get mad fast. My brother said something like that once while we were standing by his truck, and I just remember the sun was in my eyes and I was thinking about how dirty the windshield was instead of listening to him… but yeah, I got sharp with people. Like I couldn’t stand being told to ease up.\n\nDoctor: Did that fired-up, irritable period last for four days or longer, or did it fade more quickly?\n\nPatient: It didn’t just fade in a day or two, no. It’s hard for me to pin it down cleanly because everything kind of ran together after I got fired… like one week I’m up and driven and mad at everybody, and then it’s like I fell through the floor.\n\nThat whole scary swing—from that fired-up feeling to being this low—has been going on about a month, I think. Maybe around there.\n\nDoctor: Have you had three or more separate periods in your life where you felt unusually high, overly energetic, or irritable, where you needed less sleep or your thoughts raced?\n\nPatient: Three or more… I don’t know.
